: Update

\displaystyle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})\leftarrow\argmin_{p\in\mathcal{P}(\bar{x},\bar{a})}\penalty\ \left\langle p,\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\left(\bar{x},\bar{a}\right)\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot D_{\mathsf{ne}}\left(p\penalty\ \|\penalty\ \widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})\right)

Return

(\ell,\widehat{M},\mathcal{P},\{\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]})
where
\ell
is the maximum layer s.t.
(\bar{x},\bar{a})\in\mathcal{X}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{A}
was updated in
line
15
.

8.3.3
Refit
Subroutine

Now we discuss
Refit
. The skeleton is the same as in
Refit.D
: once the transition functions for
\widehat{M}
have been estimated for a given layer
h
,
Refit
attempts to compute a set of valid test policies
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}
for pairs of observations (see
Definition
8.7
). If it cannot, this implies that at least one transition that we previously estimated in layer
h
onward must have been incorrectly estimated, and we search for it starting in
line
11
. In this case, we revisit the maximum layer where some transition was updated and restart the decoding procedure.

OMD Regret as a Potential Function.

Our main innovation to control the number of refitting iterations is to design the right potential function. In
PLHR.D
, since we were working with deterministic transitions, we used the size of
\mathcal{P}(s,a)
as the potential function. Since we are now estimating
\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,a)
in a continuous space, this idea does not extend.

Instead, we use the regret of online mirror descent (OMD) against the competitor vector
\mathsf{Proj}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a))
as the potential function. We show that every transition in
line
15
witnesses constant regret with respect to
\mathsf{Proj}(P(\cdot\mid x,a))
. In our analysis, we maintain the invariant property that
\mathcal{P}
is just big enough so that
\mathsf{Proj}(P(\cdot\mid x,a))\in\mathcal{P}
throughout the execution of
PLHR
. Therefore, the standard analysis of OMD (e.g.,
Theorem
A.2
) gives us an upper bound on the cumulative regret. Letting
T_{\mathrm{refit}}
denote the number of updates on a given
(x,a)
pair, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\cdot T_{\mathrm{refit}}\lesssim\text{Regret of OMD}\lesssim\sqrt{\log\lvert\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\rvert\cdot T_{\mathrm{refit}}}.

Rearranging, we get a bound on the number of updates
T_{\mathrm{refit}}
for any
(x,a)
, and since the total number of states in the policy emulator
\widehat{M}
is bounded, we get a bound on the total number of updates made by
Refit
.

8.4  Open Problem: Generalizing
Theorem
8.1

We state the following open problem, which might be of interest to the reader.

Question 8.1
.

Can we extend
Theorem
8.1
to more general settings?

The ultimate result here would be to give a sample-efficient learning algorithm for hybrid resets which works when supplied any exploratory reset satisfying bounded concentrability.

Concretely, we believe that the technique of constructing policy emulators can be greatly generalized and simplified. As a starting point, we can show that any (pushforward) coverable MDP admits a policy emulator of bounded size (see
Section
8.4.1
for more details). However, we do not know how to efficiently construct such an emulator in the general setting. One natural class of problems to study is the low-rank MDP, which generalizes the Block MDP and also satisfies low (pushforward) coverability. An algorithm achieving
\mathrm{poly}(d)
sample complexity would showcase the power of hybrid resets, as prior work
[
SDMMS21
]
shows that
\exp(d)
sample complexity is necessary and sufficient for agnostic RL in low-rank MDPs with just online access.

Another direction for improving
Theorem
8.1
is replacing the dependence on pushforward concentrability with the (smaller) concentrability. Unfortunately, our guarantee for
PLHR
breaks down because it uses pushforward concentrability to enable accurate policy emulation of the transitions from every state in the emulator.

8.4.1  Existence of Emulators Under Pushforward Coverability

A natural question to ask is how to generalize
PLHR
beyond the Block MDP setting. As a starting point for this future research direction, we can show that every pushforward coverable MDP admits a policy emulator with a bounded state space size. We first define pushforward coverability which posits the existence of a good distribution satisfying pushforward concentrability (c.f.
Definition
2.2
).

Definition 8.4

(Pushforward Coverability
[
MFR24
,
AFJKM24
]
)
.

The pushforward coverabilty coefficient for an MDP
M
is

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{push\_cov}}(M)\coloneqq\max_{h\in[H]}\inf_{\mu_{h}\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}_{h})}\sup_{(x,a,x^{\prime})\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{X}_{h}}\frac{P(x^{\prime}\mid x,a)}{\mu_{h}(x^{\prime})}.

When clear from the context we denote the pushforward concentrability coefficient as
C_{\mathsf{push\_cov}}
.

Proposition 8.1

(Pushforward Coverable MDPs
\Rightarrow
Small Policy Emulators)
.

Let
M
be an MDP with pushforward coverability coefficient
C_{\mathsf{push\_cov}}
and
\Pi
be any policy class. Then there exists a policy emulator
\widehat{M}
with state space size

\displaystyle\mathrm{poly}\left(C_{\mathsf{push\_cov}},A,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\lvert\Pi\rvert,\log\delta^{-1}\right).

A few remarks:

•

Strictly speaking, the policy emulator we construct in
Proposition
8.1
is not a true MDP, since our construction requires the “transition”
\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})
to be an unnormalized measure over the the states in the next layer
\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]
, which may sum to
C_{\mathsf{push\_cov}}\geq 1
. Thus, we slightly abuse the notation for expectation:

\displaystyle\En_{x\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})}\left[V^{\pi}(x)\right]\coloneqq\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]}\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})V^{\pi}(x).

As discussed, the policy emulator anyways is not guaranteed to be a reasonable approximation of the underlying MDP
M
, just an object which enables uniform policy evaluation, so this issue is minor.

•

Lemma 3.1 of
[
AFJKM24
]
give a result of similar flavor, which shows that pushforward coverable MDPs are approximately
low-rank
. Their proof, however, seems to be quite different. It relies on the Johnson-Lindenstrauss lemma to construct random embeddings which enable approximation of the Bellman backup operator for any arbitrary value function class
\mathcal{F}
.

•

Unfortunately, we do not know how to leverage hybrid resets to construct such a policy emulator in a statistically efficient manner—the naive way to do so requires sample complexity scaling with
\mathfrak{C}(\Pi)
(which could be much larger than
C_{\mathsf{push\_cov}}
). We believe this is an interesting direction for future work.

Proof of
Proposition
8.1
.

We will prove this by explicitly constructing the policy emulator using the same algorithmic template as in
PLHR
. To construct the state space of the policy emulator, we sample

\widetilde{O}(C_{\mathsf{push}}/\varepsilon^{2})
observations per layer from the distributions
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{H}
, respectively, that witnesses pushforward coverability at every
h\in[H]
. As shown before, the instantaneous rewards
\widehat{R}(x,a)
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
of the emulator can be learned via the local simulator up to
\varepsilon
accuracy. Now we show that it is possible to define the transition functions
\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,a)
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
so that the resulting
\widehat{M}
is an
O(\varepsilon)
-accurate policy emulator for
d_{1}
. We do this inductively:

Claim 6
.

Let
\Gamma_{h}>0
. Suppose that at layer
h\in[H]
:

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}],\penalty\ \forall\penalty\ \pi\in\Pi:\quad\left\lvert V^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h}.

Then for every
(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})\in\mathcal{X}_{h-1}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
, there exists some
\widehat{P}\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}])
such that

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ \pi\in\Pi:\quad\left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})-\widehat{R}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})-\En_{x\sim\widehat{P}}[\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)]\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h}+2\varepsilon.

Applying this claim backwards from
h=H,\cdots,1
and using the fact that
\Gamma_{H}=\varepsilon
proves
Proposition
8.1
.

It remains to prove the claim. Let
\widehat{P}
be an unnormalized measure over
\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]
(to be defined later). First, we apply the decomposition

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})-\widehat{R}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})-\En_{x\sim\widehat{P}}[\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)]\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \left\lvert R(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})-\widehat{R}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})\right\rvert+\left\lvert\En_{x\sim P}[V^{\pi}(x)]-\En_{x\sim\widehat{P}}\left[V^{\pi}(x)\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\widehat{P}}[V^{\pi}(x)]-\En_{x\sim\widehat{P}}[\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\ \Gamma_{h}+\varepsilon+\left\lvert\En_{x\sim P}[V^{\pi}(x)]-\En_{x\sim\widehat{P}}[V^{\pi}(x)]\right\rvert,

(8.8)

where the last inequality uses the reward estimation accuracy and the assumption in the claim. To control the last term, we apply a change of measure:

\displaystyle\En_{x\sim P}\left[V^{\pi}(x)\right]=\En_{x\sim\mu_{h}}\left[\frac{P(x\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})}{\mu_{h}(x)}\cdot V^{\pi}(x)\right].

Observe that
\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]=\{x_{h}^{(1)},\cdots,x_{h}^{(n)}\}
are drawn i.i.d. from
\mu_{h}
, and by pushforward coverability, the importance ratio
P(x\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})/\mu_{h}(x)\leq C_{\mathsf{push\_cov}}
. Via a standard uniform convergence bound, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for every
\pi\in\Pi

\displaystyle\bigg|\En_{x\sim\mu_{h}}\left[\frac{P(x\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})}{\mu_{h}(x)}\cdot V^{\pi}(x)\right]-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{\frac{P(x_{h}^{(i)}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})}{n\cdot\mu_{h}(x_{h}^{(i)})}}_{\eqqcolon\widehat{P}(x_{h}^{(i)})}\cdot V^{\pi}(x_{h}^{(i)})\bigg|\leq\varepsilon.

Plugging back our choice of
\widehat{P}
into Eq. (
8.8
) proves the claim.
∎

8.5  Deferred Proofs

8.5.1  Proof for the Warmup Algorithm
PLHR.D

In this section, we prove the following sample complexity guarantee for
PLHR.D
:

Theorem
8.2
.

Let
\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
be given and suppose that
Assumption
2
holds. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,
PLHR.D
(
Algorithm
9
) finds an
\varepsilon
-optimal policy using

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{S^{5}A^{2}H^{5}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\quad\text{samples.}

8.5.1.1  Proof of
Theorem
8.2

Our high-level strategy is to apply the inductive argument outlined in
Section
8.2.2
to control the growth of the Bellman error for all
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}
as we construct
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
from layer
H
backwards. Recall our Bellman error decomposition:

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q^{\pi}(s,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)\right\rvert\leq\underbrace{\left\lvert R_{\mathsf{lat}}-\widehat{R}_{\mathsf{lat}}\right\rvert}_{\text{reward error}}+\underbrace{\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})\right\rvert}_{\text{transition error}}+\underbrace{\left\lvert V^{\pi}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})\right\rvert}_{\text{error at next layer}}.

(
8.3
)

To control the transition error of Eq. (
8.3
), we introduce a notion of test policy validity and give a lemma which shows that if
Decoder.D
is equipped with valid test policies, the transition estimation error can be bounded.

Definition 8.5

(Test Policy Validity, Deterministic Version)
.

Let
\eta>0
be a parameter. At layer
h\in[H]
, we say a collection of partial policies
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}=\{\pi_{s,s^{\prime}}\in\Pi_{h:H}:s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{h}\}
is an
\eta
-
valid test policy set
for the estimated latent MDP
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
if for every
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{h}
:

•

(Maximally distinguishing):
\pi_{s,s^{\prime}}=\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi_{h:H}}\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s^{\prime})\rvert
.

•

(Accurate):
\lvert V^{\pi_{s,s^{\prime}}}(s)-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s,s^{\prime}}}(s)\rvert\leq\eta
and
\lvert V^{\pi_{s,s^{\prime}}}(s^{\prime})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s,s^{\prime}}}(s^{\prime})\rvert\leq\eta
.

Lemma 8.1

(Decoding)
.

Fix any layer
h\in[H-1]
. Suppose that
Decoder.D
(
Algorithm
10
) is equipped with a
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}
-valid test policy
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h+1}
. Fix any tuple
(s_{h},a_{h})
and assume that
P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s_{h},a_{h})\in\mathcal{P}(s_{h},a_{h})
. With high probability,
Decoder.D
returns an updated
\mathcal{P}
such that:

1.

P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s_{h},a_{h})\in\mathcal{P}
;

2.

For every
\bar{s}\in\mathcal{P}
we have
\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s_{h},a_{h}))-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(\bar{s})\rvert\leq 7\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/2
.

The proof of
Lemma
8.1
is deferred to
Section
8.5.1.2
.

In light of
Lemma
8.1
, as long as we have valid test policy sets
\{\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
,
Lemma
8.1
provides control on the transition estimation error, and we can iteratively apply Eq. (
8.3
) to get the final bound on estimation error at layer 1.

Computing Test Policies via
Refit.D
.

Now we will analyze
Refit.D
. By standard concentration arguments, if
line
8
is triggered, the test policies must be
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}
-accurate; furthermore, they are maximally distinguishing by construction. Unfortunately, since we require the test policies to satisfy a higher level of accuracy
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}
, due to estimation errors in
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
, it may not be possible to find any valid test policies. To address this, we observe that inaccurate test policies act as a “certificate” and allow us to search for some transition
\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}\neq P_{\mathsf{lat}}
.

Lemma 8.2

(Refitting)
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
be given. Suppose that at layer
h\in[H]
,
Refit.D
(
Algorithm
11
) is supplied confidence sets
\mathcal{P}
such that for all
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h:H}\times\mathcal{A}
we have
P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s,a)\in\mathcal{P}(s,a)
. If
Refit.D
terminates at
line
17
, then with high probability:

1.

At least one
\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}
was removed from its confidence set
\mathcal{P}
.

2.

No ground truth transitions
P_{\mathsf{lat}}
are removed from their confidence set
\mathcal{P}
.

The proof of
Lemma
8.2
is deferred to
Section
8.5.1.2
.

Our analysis will track the invariant that the confidence sets
\mathcal{P}
always contain the ground truth transition
P_{\mathsf{lat}}
. Therefore,
Lemma
8.2
allows us to use the size of the confidence sets as a potential function: if
Refit.D
fails to compute valid test policies at some layer
h
, we must delete some incorrect transition
\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}
from its set
\mathcal{P}
; this process cannot continue indefinitely, since we can delete at most
S(S-1)A
states.

Proof by Induction.

With
Lemma
8.1
and
Lemma
8.2
in hand, we can show the final bound in
Theorem
8.2
. For technical convenience, we will show that the policy returned by
PLHR.D
is
O(\varepsilon)
suboptimal; rescaling the parameter
\varepsilon
does not change the final sample complexity apart from constant factors. Also, we omit the standard arguments (via concentration and union bound) which show that the conclusions of
Lemma
8.1
and
Lemma
8.2
hold with probability at least
1-\delta
over the randomness of sampling episodes from the MDP.

Take
\Gamma_{h}\coloneqq C(H-h+1)/H\cdot\varepsilon
for some suitably large constant
C>0
. We will inductively show that these properties hold for all layers
h\in[H]
:

1.

Policy Evaluation Accuracy.
For all pairs
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}
and
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathsf{open}}
:
\lvert Q^{\pi}(s,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h}
.

2.

Confidence Set Validity.
For all pairs
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h}\times\mathcal{A}
, we have
P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s,a)\in\mathcal{P}(s,a)
.

3.

Test Policy Validity.

\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}
are
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}
-valid for
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
at layer
h
.

To analyze
PLHR.D
, we will show that these properties always hold throughout at the end of every while loop for all layers
h>\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
.

Base Case.
We analyze the first loop with
\ell=H
. Note that (A) holds by concentration of the reward estimates, and (B) trivially holds because there are no transitions to be constructed at layer
H
. Now we investigate what happens when
Refit.D
is called. The computed test policies take the form
\pi_{s,s^{\prime}}\equiv a
for some
a\in\mathcal{A}
; again by concentration of the reward estimates,
line
8
of
Refit.D
is triggered. Therefore (A)–(C) hold after refitting, and we jump to
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}=H-1
.

Inductive Step.
Suppose the current layer index is
\ell
, and that properties (A)–(C) hold for all
h>\ell
. By
Lemma
8.1
, the updated transition confidence sets returned by
Decoder.D
at layer
\ell
will satisfy (B). Furthermore, at the end of
line
10
, the error decomposition (
8.3
) implies that for every
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert Q^{\pi}(s,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{\ell+1}+\frac{\varepsilon}{H^{2}}+\frac{7\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2}\leq\Gamma_{\ell},\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\text{Property (A) holds at layer $\ell$.}

Now we do casework on the outcome of
Refit.D
.

•

Case 1: Return in
line
8
.
By construction, property (C) is satisfied for layer
\ell
. In this case, since
Algorithm
11
made no updates to
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
or
\mathcal{P}
, properties (A) and (B) continue to hold at layer
\ell
onwards.

•

Case 2: Return in
line
17
.
By
Lemma
8.2
, any updates to
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
maintain property (B). Let
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
denote the layer at which we jump to. By definition of
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
, we made no updates to
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
at layers
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}+1
onwards, and therefore the previously computed test policies
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{\ell_{\mathsf{next}}+1:H}
must still be valid, so therefore properties (A) and (C) continue to hold at layer
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
onwards.

Continuing the induction, once
\ell\leftarrow 0
is reached in
PLHR.D
(which we know will eventually happen because Case 2 can only occur for
S^{2}A
times), the estimated latent MDP
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
must satisfy the bound

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert V^{\pi}(s_{1})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s_{1})\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{1}=O(\varepsilon).

Sample Complexity Bound.

We now compute the final sample complexity required by
PLHR.D
:

•

Estimating rewards in the main algorithm uses
\widetilde{O}(H^{4}SA/\varepsilon^{2})
samples.

•

Decoder.D
is called at most
SA\times S^{2}A
times, since we (re-)decode every transition
(s,a)
at most
S^{2}A
times. Every call to
Decoder.D
uses
\widetilde{O}(S^{2}/\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}^{2})=\widetilde{O}(S^{2}H^{2}/\varepsilon^{2})
samples since we take
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}=2^{5}\cdot\varepsilon/H
in
Lemma
8.2
. Therefore the total number of samples used by
Decoder.D
is at most
\widetilde{O}(S^{5}A^{2}H^{2}/\varepsilon^{2})
.

•

Refit.D
is called at most
S^{2}AH
times, since associated to every layer revisiting is an additional
H
calls in the main while loop. In every call to
Refit.D
, we use
\widetilde{O}(S^{2}H^{2}/\varepsilon^{2})
calls to compute and verify the test policy set in
line
5
. In addition, every time
line
10
is triggered corresponds to at least one deletion in
line
16
, so the number of additional samples used by
line
13
(across all calls to
Refit.D
) can be bounded by
\widetilde{O}(S^{2}AH^{3}/\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}^{2})=\widetilde{O}(S^{2}AH^{5}/\varepsilon^{2})
.

Thus the final sample complexity is at most
\widetilde{O}(S^{5}A^{2}H^{5}/\varepsilon^{2})
samples. ∎

8.5.1.2  Proof of Induction Lemmas

Proof of
Lemma
8.1
.

First we prove implication (1). Let us denote
s^{\star}=P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s_{h},a_{h})
. If
s^{\star}\notin\mathcal{P}
(the returned set), then there exists some
s^{\prime}
for which

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{h+1}\mid\pi_{s^{\star},s^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s^{\star},s^{\prime}}}(s^{\star})\right\rvert\geq 2\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}.

However, by assumption of test policy accuracy we know that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{s^{\star},s^{\prime}}}(s^{\star})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s^{\star},s^{\prime}}}(s^{\star})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}.

Since the quantity
V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{h+1}\mid\pi_{s^{\star},s^{\prime}})
is an unbiased estimate of
V^{\pi_{s^{\star},s^{\prime}}}(s^{\star})
which is estimated to accuracy
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/2
we have a contradiction, so
s^{\star}\in\mathcal{P}
.

Now we prove implication (2). If
\bar{s}\in\mathcal{P}
, then we must have

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{h+1}\mid\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}}}(\bar{s})\right\rvert=\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(s^{\star}\mid\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}}}(\bar{s})\right\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}.

Since we estimated
V_{\mathsf{mc}}(s^{\star}\mid\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}})
up to
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/2
accuracy we know that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}}}(s^{\star})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}}}(\bar{s})\right\rvert\leq 5\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/2,\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}}}(s^{\star})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{s^{\star},\bar{s}}}(\bar{s})\right\rvert\leq 7\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/2,

where the implication follows by the accuracy of
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h+1}
.
By the maximal distinguishing property of
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h+1}
, observe that the LHS of the above implication is equal to
\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s^{\star})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(\bar{s})\rvert
. This proves the second implication, and concludes the proof of
Lemma
8.1
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma
8.2
.

We show the first implication. Let
(s_{h},\pi)
be any policy which satisfies
\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(s_{h}\mid\pi)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s_{h})\rvert\geq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}-\varepsilon/H
. Since we estimated
V^{\pi}(s_{h})
up to
\varepsilon/H
error, we have
\lvert V^{\pi}(s_{h})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s_{h})\rvert\geq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}-2\varepsilon/H
. Let
\bar{s}_{h}=s_{h},\bar{s}_{h+1},\cdots,\bar{s}_{H}
be the sequence of states which are obtained by running
\pi
on
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
starting at
s_{h}
.

For sake of contradiction suppose that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{s}\mid\pi)-\widehat{R}(\bar{s},\pi)-V_{\mathsf{mc}}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)\mid\pi)\right\rvert\leq\frac{4\varepsilon}{H^{2}},\quad\text{for all}\penalty\ \bar{s}\in\{\bar{s}_{h},\cdots,\bar{s}_{H}\}.

Since we estimated every
V_{\mathsf{mc}}(\cdot\mid\pi)
up to accuracy
\varepsilon/H^{2}
we see that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s})-\widehat{R}(\bar{s},\pi)-V^{\pi}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi))\right\rvert\leq\frac{6\varepsilon}{H^{2}},\quad\text{for all}\penalty\ \bar{s}\in\{\bar{s}_{h},\cdots,\bar{s}_{H}\}.

By Performance Difference Lemma and applying the previous display recursively,

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi}(s_{h})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s_{h})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s}_{h})-\widehat{R}(\bar{s}_{h},\pi)-V^{\pi}(\bar{s}_{h+1})\right\rvert+\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s}_{h+1})-\widehat{V}(\bar{s}_{h+1})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\varepsilon}{H^{2}}+\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s}_{h+1})-\widehat{V}(\bar{s}_{h+1})\right\rvert\leq\cdots\leq\frac{6\varepsilon}{H}.

This contradicts the statement that
\left\lvert V^{\pi}(s_{h})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(s_{h})\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}-2\varepsilon/H
by the choice of
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}
. So we can conclude that there exists a state
\bar{s}\in\{\bar{s}_{h},\cdots,\bar{s}_{H}\}
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{s}\mid\pi)-\widehat{R}(\bar{s},\pi)-V_{\mathsf{mc}}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)\mid\pi)\right\rvert\geq\frac{4\varepsilon}{H^{2}},

so therefore
line
16
is executed at least once, proving the first implication.

To prove the second implication, consider any
(\bar{s},\pi)
for which
line
16
is executed. We know that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s})-\widehat{R}(\bar{s},\pi)-V^{\pi}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi))\right\rvert\geq\frac{2\varepsilon}{H^{2}}.

Recall that for all
(s,a)
, the estimation error on the rewards was
\lvert R(s,a)-\widehat{R}(s,a)\rvert\leq\varepsilon/H^{2}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s})-R(\bar{s},\pi)-V^{\pi}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi))\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{H^{2}},\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)\neq P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi).

Therefore as claimed we always delete
\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)\neq P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)
in
line
16
of
Refit.D
.
∎

8.5.2  Proof of Main Upper Bound

In this section, we prove
Theorem
8.1
.

8.5.2.1  Preliminaries

We will define some additional concepts and notation which will be used in the analysis.

•

For any set
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
we denote the represented states as
\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]\coloneqq\{\phi(x):x\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\}
. For any latent state
s\in\mathcal{S}
and subset
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
we let
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]\coloneqq\lvert\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:\phi(x)=s\}\rvert
count the total number of observations there are emitted from
s
.

•

We define the set of
\varepsilon
-pushforward-reachable latent states

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\coloneqq\left\{s_{h}:\max_{s_{h-1},a_{h-1}}P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s_{h}\mid s_{h-1},a_{h-1})\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{S}\right\},

and furthermore let
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\coloneqq\cup_{h=1}^{H}\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
.

•

For any
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, we let
n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]\coloneqq\lvert\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:\phi(x)=\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\}\rvert
and
n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]=\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rvert-n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]
.

Estimated Transitions and Projected Measures.

Recall that the ground truth latent transition is denoted
P_{\mathsf{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\to\Delta(\mathcal{S})
. We will use
\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}
to denote the empirical version of the latent transition which is sampled in
line
3
of
Algorithm
13
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\phi(x_{h}),a_{h})=\frac{1}{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\delta_{\phi(x)}.

In addition, we introduce a notion of projected measures which will be used to relate the ground truth transition
P=\psi\circ P_{\mathsf{lat}}
with the estimated transition
\widehat{P}
of the policy emulator. While our algorithm never directly uses the projected measure, we track it in the analysis.

Definition 8.6

(Projected Measure)
.

For a distribution
p\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})
, define the
projected measure
onto the observation set
\bar{\mathcal{X}}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)\coloneqq\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}p(s)\cdot\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\bar{\mathcal{X}}:\phi(x)=s\}).

Specifically, for any
x\in\bar{\mathcal{X}}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)(x)=p(\phi(x))\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}]}.

Furthermore, for any subset
\bar{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{X}}
we denote
\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)(\bar{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})=\sum_{x\in\bar{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)(x)
.

Formally,
\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}
is not a true probability distribution, as the total measure might not sum up to 1. This would happen if
p(s)>0
for
s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}
.

Remark.
In
Theorem
8.1
, we assume that the distribution
\mu
is factorizable. This can be removed with some extra work. One can modify the definition of the projected measure to replace the uniform distribution over observations with some other suitable importance-reweighted distribution; the existence of such distribution with desirable properties that allow concentration of the pushforward policies can be shown using pushforward concentrability (i.e., in
Lemma
8.4
).

Test Policy Validity.

In our analysis, we will modify
Definition
8.5
as below.

Definition 8.7

(Valid Test Policy)
.

For a layer
h\in[H]
, we say a collection of policies
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}=\left\{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}\right\}_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]}

is a
\eta
-
valid test policy set
for policy emulator
\widehat{M}
if the following hold.

•

(Maximally distinguishing):
\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}=\argmax_{\pi\in\mathcal{A}\circ\Pi_{h+1:H}}\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert
.

•

(Accurate): For all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\eta\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\eta.

8.5.2.2  Supporting Technical Lemmas for Sampling

In this section, we establish several technical lemmas which show that various conditions that we need in the analysis hold with high probability under samples from
M
.

Properties of Policy Emulator Initialization.

We prove several properties that hold with high probability when the policy emulator is initialized in
line
4
-
8
of
Algorithm
12
.

Lemma 8.3

(Sampling of Pushforward-Reachable States)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ h\in[H],\forall\penalty\ s\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}:\quad n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]]\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2C_{\mathsf{push}}S}\cdot n_{\mathsf{reset}}.

Proof.

Fix any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
. For any
i\in[n_{\mathsf{reset}}]
, let
Z^{(i)}
be the indicator variable of whether observation
x^{(i)}_{h}\sim\mu_{h}
satisfies
\phi(x^{(i)}_{h})=s
. We know that
\En[Z^{(i)}]\geq\varepsilon/(C_{\mathsf{push}}S)
. By Chernoff bounds we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\frac{1}{n_{\mathsf{reset}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathsf{reset}}}Z^{(i)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{\mathsf{push}}S}\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}\cdot\varepsilon}{8C_{\mathsf{push}}S}\right),

so as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{reset}}\geq\frac{8C_{\mathsf{push}}S}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{SH}{\delta},

by union bound, the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma 8.4

(Pushforward Policy Concentration over
\mu
)
.

Suppose that the conclusion of
Lemma
8.3
holds. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ h\in[H],

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ (s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\times\mathcal{A}:

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})\right](a)\right\rvert\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}.

Proof.

Fix any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\times\mathcal{A}
. Also fix any policy
\pi\in\Pi
. Denote the set
\mathcal{X}_{s}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\}
, and observe that
\mathcal{X}_{s}
is drawn i.i.d. from the emission distribution
\psi(s)
. By Hoeffding bounds we have

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\Pr\left[\left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})\right](a)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]]\varepsilon^{2}}{A^{2}}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}\varepsilon^{3}}{C_{\mathsf{push}}SA^{2}}\right),

(
Lemma
8.3
)

Applying union bound we see that as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{reset}}\geq\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}SA^{2}}{\varepsilon^{3}}\cdot\log\frac{2SAH\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}

the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma 8.5

(Sampling Rewards)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, every reward estimate
\widehat{R}(x,a)
computed in
line
7
of
Algorithm
12
satisfies
\lvert\widehat{R}(x,a)-R(x,a)\rvert\leq\varepsilon/H
.

Proof.

This follows by Hoeffding inequality and union bound over all
n_{\mathsf{reset}}\cdot AH
pairs
(x,a)\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Properties of
Decoder
.

Now we turn to analyzing a single call to
Decoder
.

Lemma 8.6

(Sampling Transitions)
.

Fix any
(x_{h},a_{h})
for which we call
Decoder
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, the dataset
\mathcal{D}
sampled in
line
3
of
Algorithm
13
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

Every time a dataset
\mathcal{D}
is sampled, by concentration of discrete distributions we have for any
t>0
:

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\left\|P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right\|_{1}\geq\sqrt{S}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}}+t\right)\right]\leq\exp(-n_{\mathsf{dec}}t^{2}).

Setting the RHS to
\delta
we have that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta)}{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}}.

Therefore as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{dec}}\geq\frac{S}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log\frac{1}{\delta},

the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Corollary 3
.

If the conclusion of
Lemma
8.6
holds, then the proportion of observations from
(\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}
in
\mathcal{D}
is at most
2\varepsilon
.

Lemma 8.7

(Pushforward Policy Concentration over Transitions)
.

Fix any
(x_{h},a_{h})
for which we call
Decoder
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, the dataset
\mathcal{D}
sampled in
line
3
of
Algorithm
13
satisfies

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{D}],

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ a\in\mathcal{A},\forall\penalty\ \pi\in\Pi:

\displaystyle\Big|\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{D}:\phi(x)=s\right\}\right)\right](a)\Big|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert/\delta)}{n_{s}[\mathcal{D}]}}.

Proof.

Fix a particular
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{D}]
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
, and
\pi\in\Pi
. The set
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\}
is drawn i.i.d. from the emission distribution
\psi(s)
. By Hoeffding bounds we have for any
t>0
:

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\Big|\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{D}:\phi(x)=s\right\}\right)\right](a)\Big|\geq t\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-2n_{s}[\mathcal{D}]t^{2}\right).

By union bound over all
(s,a)
and
\pi
, with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\Big|\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{D}:\phi(x)=s\right\}\right)\right](a)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert/\delta)}{n_{s}[\mathcal{D}]}}

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.8

(Monte Carlo Estimates for
Decoder
)
.

Fix any
(x_{h},a_{h})
for which we call
Decoder
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, every Monte Carlo estimate
V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x\mid\pi)
computed in
line
6
of
Algorithm
13
satisfies

\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x\mid\pi)-V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon
.

Proof.

By Hoeffding’s inequality we know that for a fixed
(x,\pi)
pair:

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x\mid\pi)-V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon\right]\leq 2\exp(-2n_{\mathsf{mc}}\varepsilon^{2}).

In total, we call
line
6
at most
\lvert\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]\rvert^{2}\leq n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}
times. Therefore, by union bound, as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{mc}}\geq K\cdot\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}SAH\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\varepsilon\delta}

where
K>0
is an absolute constant determined by the value of
n_{\mathsf{reset}}
, then the result holds.
∎

Properties of
Refit
.

Now we establish the accuracy of estimates in a single call to
Refit
.

Lemma 8.9

(Monte Carlo Estimates for
Refit
)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, every Monte Carlo estimate computed by
Refit
(
line
5
and
11
of
Algorithm
14
) is accurate up to error
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

In
Refit
we compute Monte Carlo estimates for
2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}+2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{3}\cdot AH
times, since there are
2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}
possible certificates
(x,\pi)
and for each one we perform Monte Carlo estimates over all of the
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pairs in our policy emulator
\widehat{M}
. By Hoeffding bound and union bound we see that as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{mc}}\geq K\cdot\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}SAH\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\varepsilon\delta},

for some absolute constant
K>0
, the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Additional Notation.

Henceforth, let us define several events:

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}\coloneqq\left\{\text{the conclusions of \hyperref@@ii[lem:sampling-states]{Lemma\penalty\ \ref*{lem:sampling-states}} --- \ref{lem:sampling-rewards} hold}\right\}
. We have
\Pr[\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}]\geq 1-3\delta
.

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}\coloneqq\left\{\text{the conclusions of \hyperref@@ii[lem:sampling-transitions]{Lemma\penalty\ \ref*{lem:sampling-transitions}} --- \ref{lem:monte-carlo} hold on the $t$-th call to {{Decoder}}{}}\right\}
. We have
\Pr[\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}]\geq 1-3\delta
. Furthermore, define the random variable
T_{\mathsf{D}}
to be the total number of times that
Decoder
is called.

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\coloneqq\left\{\text{the conclusion of \hyperref@@ii[lem:refit-monte-carlo-accuracy]{Lemma\penalty\ \ref*{lem:refit-monte-carlo-accuracy}} holds on the $t$-th call to {{Refit}}{}}\right\}
. We have
\Pr[\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}]\geq 1-\delta
. Furthermore, define the random variable
T_{\mathsf{R}}
to be the total number of times that
Refit
is called.

In the analysis, we will drop the subscript
t
when referring to
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}
if clear from the context.

8.5.2.3  Analysis of
Decoder

This section is dedicated to establishing
Lemma
8.10
, which is the main inductive lemma.

Lemma 8.10

(Induction for
Decoder
)
.

Fix any layer
h\in[H]
and tuple
(x_{h},a_{h})
on which
Decoder
is called. Assume that:

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}
hold.

•

For all
x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]
:
\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}}\penalty\ \lvert V^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}
.

•

Input confidence set
\mathcal{P}(x_{h},a_{h})
satisfies
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h}))\in\mathcal{P}(x_{h},a_{h})
.

•

\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h+1}
are
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}
-valid test policies for the policy emulator
\widehat{M}
.

Then
Decoder
returns confidence set
\mathcal{P}
via Eq. (
8.6
) such that:

1.

\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h}))\in\mathcal{P}
;

2.

\max_{\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}}\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}}\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}+K\cdot\left(\beta+S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}\right)
.

Here,
K>0
is an absolute numerical constant.

8.5.2.3.1  Structural Properties of the Decoder Graph

For the lemmas in this section, we will assume the preconditions of
Lemma
8.10
and analyze properties of the decoder graph
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
constructed in a single call to
Decoder
.

Lemma 8.11

(Validity of Decoding Function)
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
8.10
, for every
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}
, we have

\displaystyle\{x_{r}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x_{r})=\phi(x_{l})\}\subseteq\mathcal{T}[x_{l}].

Proof.

The proof is a reprise of the argument used in Part (1) of
Lemma
8.1
. We prove this by contradiction. Suppose that there existed some
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}
and
x_{r}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}
such that
\phi(x_{l})=\phi(x_{r})
but
x_{r}\notin\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
. Then
x_{r}
must have lost a test to some other
x_{r}^{\prime}
, i.e. there exists some
x_{r}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+2\varepsilon.

(8.9)

By accuracy of
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h+1}
and the fact that
\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}
is open-loop at layer
h+1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{l})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert=\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}.

(8.10)

Furthermore, by
Lemma
8.8
we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert=\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{l})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon.

(8.11)

Combining (
8.10
) and (
8.11
) we get that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+\varepsilon,

which contradicts (
8.9
). This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.12

(Biclique Property)
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
8.10
, for any
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]
the subgraph of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
over vertices
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}
is a biclique.

Proof.

Fix any
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]
. By
Lemma
8.11
, any
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}
such that
\phi(x_{l})=s
has an edge to every observation
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
. Therefore, the subgraph over
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}
forms a biclique in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
.
∎

Lemma 8.13
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
8.10
, for any connected component
\mathbb{C}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
.

Proof.

Fix any
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]
, and let
x_{l}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
be any arbitrary observation such that
\phi(x_{l})=s
. By
Lemma
8.3
, since
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
, there exist some
x_{r}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}
such that
\phi(x_{r})=s
; in other words,
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]
. Moreover by
Lemma
8.11
, there must be an edge from
x_{l}
to
x_{r}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
. Therefore
x_{r}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}
, so
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
. ∎

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}
such that
\phi(x)=\phi(x^{\prime})
. We have
\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}
.

Proof.

Denote
\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}=\argmax_{\pi\in\mathcal{A}\circ\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\rvert
to be the test policy for the pair
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}
. By accuracy of the test policy we know that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}.

Furthermore since
x,x^{\prime}
are observations emitted from the same latent state and
\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}
is open loop at layer
h+1
, we have
V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)=V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})
. Therefore

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x,\pi_{x,x^{\prime}})-\widehat{Q}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime},\pi_{x,x^{\prime}})\right\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

Fix any
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}
. If
x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
, then
\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r},a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r}^{\prime},a)\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+4\varepsilon
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+2\varepsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r}^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+2\varepsilon.

Using the fact that test policies are maximally distinguishing we have

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r},a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r}^{\prime},a)\right\rvert=\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r}^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+4\varepsilon.

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.16

(Bounded Width of
\mathbb{C}
)
.

For any connected component
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}_{j\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
we have

\displaystyle\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\rvert\leq 4S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon.

Proof.

Let us take any
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}
. Since
x,x^{\prime}
belong to the same connected component, there exists a sequence of observations
\mathsf{seq}=(x_{1}=x,\dots,x_{n}=x^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})^{n}
such that for every consecutive pair
x_{i},x_{i+1}
there exists some
x_{l}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
such that
x_{i},x_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
.

Fix any
a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi
. Now we will bound
\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\rvert
. We construct an auxiliary sequence
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}=(\widetilde{x}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{x}_{k})
for some
k\leq n
as follows:

•

Initialize
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}=\emptyset
.

•

For
i=1,\cdots,n
:

–

Add
x_{i}
to the end of
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
.

–

If there exists
x_{j}
with
j>i
such that
\phi(x_{i})=\phi(x_{j})
then set
i\leftarrow j
.

Observe that
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
satisfies the following conditions:

•

\widetilde{x}_{1}=x
and
\widetilde{x}_{k}=x^{\prime}
.

•

For every
s\in\mathcal{S}
, at most two observations
\widetilde{x},\widetilde{x}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{supp}(\psi(s))\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}
are found in
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
, and these observations must appear sequentially.

•

For any
i\in[k-1]
, if
\phi(\widetilde{x}_{i})\neq\phi(\widetilde{x}_{i+1})
then there exists some
x_{l}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
such that
\widetilde{x}_{i},\widetilde{x}_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
.

Now we can apply triangle inequality to
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(\widetilde{x}_{i},a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(\widetilde{x}_{i+1},a)\right\rvert\leq 4S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon.

The final bound uses the aforementioned properties of
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
, as well as
Lemma
8.14
and
Lemma
8.15
to handle the individual terms in the summation. This completes the proof of
Lemma
8.16
.
∎

8.5.2.3.2  Structural Properties of Projected Measures

Now we will prove several lemmas regarding the projected measure of the empirical latent distribution

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}=\frac{1}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}}\delta_{\phi(x)}.

which is sampled in
line
3
of a single call to
Decoder
.

Lemma 8.17
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
8.10
, for any connected component
\mathbb{C}
of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s).

Proof.

We compute that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}.

For
(i)
, observe that if
s\notin\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
, then the sum
\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}=0
. For
(ii)
, we use the fact that
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]=0
if
s\notin\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]
. From here, we apply the biclique lemma (
Lemma
8.12
). The biclique lemma implies that if
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
, then
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
, and therefore
\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]=\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
. Furthermore for any
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
, all of the observations
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}
, so
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]=n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
. Thus we can continue the calculation as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s).

This concludes the proof of
Lemma
8.17
.
∎

Corollary 4
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
8.10
, then

\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}(\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}))\in[0,2\varepsilon]
.

Proof.

For any
\mathbb{C}
we have

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

(
Lemma
8.17
)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

(
Lemma
8.13
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}+\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}.

The last equality uses the fact that by
Lemma
8.13
,
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\Rightarrow s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
, so in particular by
Lemma
8.12
we have
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
, so therefore
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]=n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]
.

Summing over all
\mathbb{C}
and applying
Corollary
3
we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})=\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}=\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\in[0,2\varepsilon].

This proves
Corollary
4
.
∎

Lemma 8.18
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
8.10
, for every
\pi\in\Pi
and
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
:

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\penalty\ \left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\right\rvert\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]},

where
K>0
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

Fix any
\pi\in\Pi
,
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
, and
a\in\mathcal{A}
. We can calculate that:

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right).

The last line uses the biclique lemma (
Lemma
8.12
) in the same fashion as the proof of
Lemma
8.17
. Now we apply the conclusions of
Lemma
8.4
and
Lemma
8.7
, along with the fact that for every
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]
we have
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}
as well as
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
(which is again implied by the biclique lemma):

\displaystyle\hskip-15.00002pt\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)+\frac{\varepsilon}{A}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+\sqrt{\frac{2\log\tfrac{2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt+\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\Bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{A}\cdot n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]+\sqrt{2n_{s}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\log\tfrac{2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt+\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\Bigg)

By
Lemma
8.17
, we have
\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]=n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]
. Using Cauchy-Schwarz we get that

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{1}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\cdot\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

(8.12)

Let us investigate the last term. We have

\displaystyle\hskip-40.00006pt\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

(
Lemma
8.13
)

\displaystyle\leq\quad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)

Plugging this back into Eq. (
8.12
) we get that

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\cdot\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})\right](a),

and rearranging and using the fact that
\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\in[0,1]
we get that

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}.

One can repeat the same steps to get the lower bound. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\right\rvert\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}.

This proves
Lemma
8.18
.
∎

8.5.2.3.3  Proof of
Lemma
8.10

Fix the
(x_{h},a_{h})
pair on which we call
Decoder
.

For notational convenience we will denote
\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}\coloneqq\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]
, as well as use
P_{\mathsf{lat}}=P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\phi(x_{h}),a_{h})
to denote the ground truth latent transition function. Throughout the proof, we use
K>0
to denote absolute constants whose values may change line-by-line.

Part (1).

Since the input confidence set
\mathcal{P}
satisfies the third bullet, it suffices to show that that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})
satisfies both of the constraints in the confidence set construction of Eq. (
8.6
).

For the first constraint, observe that by
Corollary
4
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon.

Therefore it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon.

We calculate that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\left(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))\right)\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\left(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\right)\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\left\lvert P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon.

(
Lemma
8.6
)

(8.13)

Now we prove that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})
also satisfies the second constraint, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon.

Observe that we can break up the bound as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\underbrace{\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{2}}.

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{1}
.
To bound
\mathtt{Term}_{1}
, we compute:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}<4\varepsilon}\frac{2\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}+(8S+4)\varepsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+A\cdot\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\right)+(8S+5)\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]+n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+A\cdot\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\right)+(8S+5)\varepsilon.

(8.14)

The inequality
(i)
follows by casework on
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
:

•

If
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\neq\emptyset
then by the biclique lemma (
Lemma
8.12
) we have
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
. In other words, all of the observations from states in
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]
are contained in this
\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
. Therefore, there can be at most
S
such components
\mathbb{C}
, and their contribution to the sum is
8\varepsilon\cdot S
.

•

If
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]=\emptyset
, then
\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
only contains observations from
(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}
, and therefore the total size of such
\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}
can be bounded by
2\varepsilon\cdot\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert
using
Corollary
3
. Their contribution to the sum is
4\varepsilon
.

Furthermore,
(ii)
uses
Lemma
8.18
.

We now proceed to separately bound the terms in Eq. (
8.14
). First, observe that

\displaystyle K\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}\cdot\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\sqrt{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{\frac{S^{2}A^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{\frac{S^{2}A^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.

(8.15)

The first inequality follows because by the biclique lemma (
Lemma
8.12
) we know that the summation must have at most
S
terms, since each of the
\mathbb{C}
contains some
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
, so we can apply Cauchy-Schwarz for
S
-dimensional vectors. The second inequality is a consequence of
Corollary
3
, and the last inequality follows by our choice of
n_{\mathsf{dec}}
.

In addition by
Corollary
3
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\leq 2\varepsilon.

(8.16)

For the other two terms, observe that by
Lemma
8.18
, when
\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon
we must have
\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\leq 1
so therefore

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\left(K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\left(K\cdot\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}\cdot\varepsilon.

(8.17)

Combining Eqns. (
8.14
), (
8.15
), (
8.16
), and (
8.17
) we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq K\left(\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}+S\right)\varepsilon.

(8.18)

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{2}
.
To bound
\mathtt{Term}_{2}
, fix any
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
. Note that

\displaystyle\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\lvert\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\left(\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\right)\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\frac{\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left\lvert\frac{\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}\left\lvert\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)-P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\cdot\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}
