\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}]}P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\left\lvert 1-\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

(
Lemma
8.6
)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\varepsilon}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}=2\varepsilon\frac{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}.

(using Eq.
(
8.13
)
)

Also, we have the trivial bound that

\displaystyle\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq 2,

because it is a difference of two probability measures, so we can write the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\wedge 2.

(8.19)

Using Eq.
(
8.19
)
we get that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(\frac{\varepsilon\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\wedge 1\right)=2\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\wedge\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]+n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}+2\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}<4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\leq(8S+8)\varepsilon.

(8.20)

The last inequality uses the facts that (1)
Corollary
3
implies that for any
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
such that
\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon
we have
\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]+n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}]}\leq 2
and (2) the same casework we showed above to handle the summation for
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
such that
\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}<4\varepsilon
.

Putting together Eqns. (
8.18
) and (
8.20
):

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}+S\right)\varepsilon\eqqcolon\beta.

Thus, we can conclude that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})\in\mathcal{P}
, thus concluding the proof of Part (1).

Part (2).

Observe that in light of Part (1), the set
\mathcal{P}
is nonempty so therefore the maximum is well defined.

We want to show a bound on

\displaystyle\max_{\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}}\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}}\penalty\ \left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert.

Fix any
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}
and
\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}
. We compute

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{H}+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim P_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

(
Lemma
8.5
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

(
Lemma
8.6
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\underbrace{\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{3}}.

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{1}
.
For the first term, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\mathtt{Term}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert=\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}\En_{x\sim\psi(s)}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\En_{x\sim\psi(s)}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\})}V^{\pi}(x)\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\left\{s\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\left(\En_{x\sim\psi(s)}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\})}V^{\pi}(x)\right)\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 3\varepsilon.

(8.21)

The first inequality follows by
Corollary
3
, and the second inequality follows by
Lemma
8.4
.

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{2}
.
For the second term, we have by assumption that:

\displaystyle\mathtt{Term}_{2}=\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\left[V^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right]\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}.

(8.22)

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{3}
.
Now we calculate a bound on
\mathtt{Term}_{3}
. In what follows for any connected component
\mathbb{C}
we let
x_{\mathbb{C}}
denote an arbitrary fixed observation from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}
(for example, the lowest indexed one). Observe that for any
p\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}})
we have

\displaystyle\En_{x\sim p}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}p(x)\cdot\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,\pi(x))\hskip 90.00014pt\text{($\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}$ form a partition of $\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}$)}

\displaystyle\leq 4S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}p(x)\cdot\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x_{\mathbb{C}}))\hskip 80.00012pt\text{(\hyperref@@ii[lem:width-of-cc]{Lemma\penalty\ \ref*{lem:width-of-cc}})}

\displaystyle=4S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}p(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{p(x)}{p(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x_{\mathbb{C}})).

Similarly, one can show the lower bound on
\En_{x\sim p}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)
. Therefore we apply the bound to get:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 8S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))-\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\bar{p}(x)}{\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}8S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+2\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))-\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}}\frac{\bar{p}(x)}{\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}8S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\lvert\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))-\frac{\bar{p}(x)}{\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\right\rvert,

\displaystyle\leq 8S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\bar{p}(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C})\right\|_{1},

where
(i)
follows by
Corollary
4
and the bound
\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\in[0,1]
, and
(ii)
follows by the first constraint on
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}
and the bound
\frac{p(x)}{p(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\in[0,1]
.

From here, we will use the second constraint on
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 8S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\bar{p}(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 8S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\beta+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathsf{L}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 8S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+2\beta.

(8.23)

The last inequality follows because our proof for Part (1) of the lemma actually showed that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})\in\mathcal{P}
. Combining Eqns. (
8.21
), (
8.22
), and (
8.23
) we get the final bound

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}+K\cdot\left(\beta+S\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}\right).

This completes the proof of
Lemma
8.10
.

8.5.2.4  Analysis of
Refit

Lemma 8.19

(Certificate Implies Transition Inaccuracy)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}
hold. Let
\widehat{M}
be a policy emulator. Suppose there exists a certificate
(x,\pi)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times(\mathcal{A}\circ\Pi_{h+1:H})
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}.

Then there exists some tuple
(\bar{x},\bar{a})\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}.

(8.24)

Proof.

Suppose that Eq. (
8.24
) did not hold for any
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
. Then by the Performance Difference Lemma we have

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert V^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\widehat{R}(x,\pi)-R(x,\pi)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}+\frac{\varepsilon}{H}+\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}+\frac{\varepsilon}{H}+\max_{x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}\left\lvert V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\cdots\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2}+\varepsilon,

where
(i)
uses
Lemma
8.5
and the negation of Eq. (
8.24
), and
(ii)
applies the bound recursively. Since
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}>2\varepsilon
, we have reached a contradiction. This proves
Lemma
8.19
.
∎

Lemma 8.20

(Refitting Correctness)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}},\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}
hold. The following are true about
Algorithm
14
in the search for incorrect transitions (
line
9
-
15
are executed):

(1)

For every
(x,\pi)
from in
line
10
, at least one such
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pair is identified by
line
13
.

(2)

Every
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pair identified by
line
13
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h(\bar{x})+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H}.

(3)

For every
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
identified by
line
13
, the corresponding loss vector
\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}
from
line
15
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h(\bar{x})+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H}.

Proof.

To prove Part (1) we use
Lemma
8.19
, which shows that there exists at least one such
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}.

(8.25)

Therefore we know that for such
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x^{\prime}\mid\pi)+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)\right\rvert+3\varepsilon

(
Lemma
8.9
and
Lemma
8.5
)

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\right\rvert+3\varepsilon

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\left\lvert\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}-3\varepsilon\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H},

(Using Eq. (
8.25
))

so therefore this
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
is identified by
line
13
of
Refit
.

Now we prove Part (2). Fix any
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pair identified by
line
13
of
Refit
. Let
h=h(\bar{x})
denote the layer that a given
\bar{x}
is found. First we observe that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})=\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\psi\circ P_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\En_{s\sim P_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\psi(s)}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]-\mathbbm{1}\left\{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\En_{s\sim P_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\left\{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\left(\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\psi(s)}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon,

where the last inequality uses
Lemma
8.4
. The other side of the inequality can be similarly shown, so

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon.

(8.26)

We can compute that

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x^{\prime}\mid\pi)+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q^{\pi}(\bar{x},\bar{a})\right\rvert+2\varepsilon

(
Lemma
8.9
)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert+3\varepsilon

(
Lemma
8.5
)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert+5\varepsilon.

(Eq. (
8.26
))

Rearranging we see that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H}-5\varepsilon\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H},

and this proves Part (2).

For Part (3), suppose that
\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\geq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/8H
(the case where
\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\leq-\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/8H
can be tackled similarly). Then we have
\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\coloneqq Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\cdot,\pi(\cdot)\mid\pi)\in[0,1]^{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}
. We can compute that

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H}

\displaystyle\leq\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(x^{\prime},\pi(x^{\prime})\mid\pi)+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a}),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a}),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q^{\pi}(\bar{x},\bar{a})

(
Lemma
8.9
)

\displaystyle\leq 4\varepsilon+\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a}),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})

(
Lemma
8.5
and Eq. (
8.26
))

\displaystyle\leq 5\varepsilon+\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle

(
Lemma
8.9
)

Rearranging we get
\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle\geq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/(16H)
, thus proving part (3).
∎

Lemma 8.21

(Bound on Number of Refits)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}},\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}
hold, and that every time
Algorithm
14
is called, the confidence sets
\mathcal{P}
satisfy

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ (x,a)\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}:\quad\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h(x)+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a))\in\mathcal{P}(x,a).

Then across all calls to
Algorithm
14
,
line
15
is executed at most
(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH/\varepsilon^{2})\cdot\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}
times.

Proof.

Fix
h\in[H]
and a pair
(x,a)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
. Suppose we execute
line
15
for
T_{\mathrm{refit}}
times on
(x,a)
. Denote the sequence of transition estimates as
\{\widehat{P}^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x,a)\}_{t\in[T_{\mathrm{refit}}]}
and the sequence of loss vectors as
\{\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\}_{t\in[T_{\mathrm{refit}}]}
.

By Part (3) of
Lemma
8.20
, for all times
t\in[T_{\mathrm{refit}}]
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{P}^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x,a)-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a)),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H}.

(8.27)

On the other hand, we have a bound on the total regret of OMD with step size
\varepsilon

[
Bub11
]
:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\mathrm{refit}}}\left\langle\widehat{P}^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x,a)-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a)),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}D_{\mathsf{ne}}\left(\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a))\penalty\ \|\penalty\ \widehat{P}^{(1)}(\cdot\mid x,a)\right)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\mathrm{refit}}}\left\|\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}}{\varepsilon}+\frac{\varepsilon T_{\mathrm{refit}}}{2}.

(8.28)

Therefore, combining Eqs. (
8.27
) and (
8.28
) along with the value
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}=80H\varepsilon
we have the bound

\displaystyle T_{\mathrm{refit}}\leq\frac{\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Using the fact that there are
n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH
such
(x,a)
pairs proves the result.
∎

8.5.2.5  Proof of
Theorem
8.1

In the proof, we will assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}
holds, that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}
holds for all times
t\in[T_{\mathsf{D}}]
, that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}
holds for all times
t\in[T_{\mathsf{R}}]
. By union bound, this holds with probability at least
1-(3T_{\mathsf{D}}+T_{\mathsf{R}}+3)\delta
.

We will show that under the choice of parameters
n_{\mathsf{reset}}
,
n_{\mathsf{dec}}
, and
n_{\mathsf{mc}}
in the algorithm pseudocode,
PLHR
returns a
\widetilde{O}(SAH^{2}\varepsilon)
-optimal policy, and that

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{D}},T_{\mathsf{R}}\leq\mathrm{poly}(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\lvert\Pi\rvert,\log\delta^{-1}).

Therefore, rescaling
\varepsilon
and
\delta
will imply the final result.

Proof by Induction.

Take
\Gamma_{h}\coloneqq K(H-h+1)(\beta+SH)\varepsilon
for some suitably large constant
K>0
. Furthermore set
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}=81H\varepsilon
. We will show that the following properties holds at every layer
h\in[H]
:

(1)

Transition set includes ground truth:

\forall\penalty\ (x,a)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
,
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a))\in\mathcal{P}(x,a)
.

(2)

Accurate value estimates:

\forall\penalty\ (x,a)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}
,
\lvert Q^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h}
.

(3)

Valid test policies:

\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}
are
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}
-valid for
\widehat{M}
at layer
h
.

To analyze
PLHR
we will show that at the end of every while loop, these properties always hold for all layers
h>\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
.

Base Case.
For the first loop with
\ell=H
, property (1) holds because there are no transitions to be constructed at layer
H
. By
Lemma
8.5
, property (2) holds after the initialization of the policy emulator in
line
8
. Furthermore, in the first call to
Refit
, the computed test policies are open loop, so again using
Lemma
8.5
, we see that
line
8
is triggered. Therefore, properties (2) and (3) hold at the end of the while loop. The current layer index is set to
\ell\leftarrow H-1
.

Inductive Step.
Suppose the current layer index is
\ell
, and that properties (1)–(3) hold for all
h>\ell
. By
Lemma
8.10
, for every
(x_{\ell},a_{\ell})
that we call
Decoder
on the updated confidence sets
\mathcal{P}
returned by satisfy property (1), and the choice
\widehat{P}\in\mathcal{P}
satisfies property (2). Now we do casework on the outcome of
Refit
called at layer
\ell
.

•

Case 1: Return in
line
8
.
By construction, property (3) is satisfied for layer
\ell
. In this case, since
Refit
made no updates to
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
or
\mathcal{P}
, properties (1) and (2) continue to hold at layer
\ell
onwards.

•

Case 2: Return in
line
16
.
Property (1) holds because
Refit
does not modify
\mathcal{P}
. Let
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
denote the layer at which we jump to.
Refit
made no updates to
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
at layers
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}+1
onwards, and therefore the previously computed test policies
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{\ell_{\mathsf{next}}+1:H}
must still be valid, so therefore properties (2) and (3) continue to hold at layer
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
onwards.

Continuing the induction, once
\ell\leftarrow 0
is reached in
PLHR
, the policy emulator
\widehat{M}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert V^{\pi}(s_{1})-\En_{x_{1}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathcal{X}_{1}[\widehat{M}])}[\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x_{1})]\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{1}\leq\widetilde{O}(SAH^{2}\varepsilon).

(8.29)

Bounding the Number of Calls to
Decoder
and
Refit
.

By
Lemma
8.21
, the total number of executions of
line
15
in
Algorithm
14
is at most
(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH/\varepsilon^{2})\cdot\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}
. In the worst case, every revisit to an already computed layer (i.e., jumping back to
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}\geq\ell
) requires us to restart
Decoder
at layer
H
and therefore decode at most
n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH
additional times, so therefore

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{D}}\leq\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}A^{2}H^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}.

Similarly, every revisit in the worst case requires
H
additional calls to
Refit
so therefore

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{R}}\leq\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}.

As claimed, both
T_{\mathsf{D}}
and
T_{\mathsf{R}}
are
\mathrm{poly}(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\lvert\Pi\rvert,\log\delta^{-1})
.

Final Sample Complexity Bound.

Now we compute the total number of samples.

•

Algorithm
12
uses
n_{\mathsf{reset}}\cdot AH
samples to
\mu_{h}
to form the state space of the policy emulator, and for each state-action pair we sample
\widetilde{O}(H^{2}\varepsilon^{-2})
times to estimate the reward.

•

Algorithm
13
is called
T_{\mathsf{D}}\leq\widetilde{O}(n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}A^{2}H^{2}\varepsilon^{-2})
times, and each time we use
n_{\mathsf{dec}}\cdot n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}n_{\mathsf{mc}}
rollouts.

•

Algorithm
14
is called
T_{\mathsf{R}}\leq\widetilde{O}(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH^{2}\varepsilon^{-2})
times, and each time we use
2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}n_{\mathsf{mc}}
to evaluate the test policies. Furthermore, by
Lemma
8.21
,
line
11
is triggered at most
\widetilde{O}(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH\varepsilon^{-2})
times, with every time requiring an additional
n_{\mathsf{mc}}\cdot n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH
rollouts.

Therefore in total we use

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003ptn_{\mathsf{reset}}\frac{AH^{3}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{4}n_{\mathsf{dec}}n_{\mathsf{mc}}\frac{A^{2}H^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{3}n_{\mathsf{mc}}\frac{AH^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}n_{\mathsf{mc}}\frac{A^{2}H^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}^{4}S^{6}A^{12}H^{3}}{\varepsilon^{18}}\cdot\mathrm{polylog}\left(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\lvert\Pi\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\delta^{-1}\right)\quad\text{samples.}

Afterwards, we can rescale
\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon/\widetilde{O}(SAH^{2})
so that the bound Eq. (
8.29
) is at most
\varepsilon
, and rescale
\delta\leftarrow\delta/(3T_{\mathsf{D}}+T_{\mathsf{R}}+1)
so that the guarantee occurs with probability at least
1-\delta
. The final sample complexity is

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}^{4}S^{24}A^{30}H^{39}}{\varepsilon^{18}}\cdot\mathrm{polylog}\left(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\lvert\Pi\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\delta^{-1}\right)\quad\text{samples.}

This concludes the proof of
Theorem
8.1
.∎

Appendix A
Technical Tools

A.1  Concentration Inequalities

Lemma A.1

(Hoeffding’s Inequality)
.

Let
Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{n}
be independent bounded random variables with
Z_{i}\in[a,b]
for all
i\in[n]
. Then

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\En[Z_{i}]\right\rvert\geq t\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2nt^{2}}{(b-a)^{2}}\right).

Lemma A.2

(Multiplicative Chernoff Bound)
.

Let
Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{n}
be i.i.d. random variables taking values in
\{0,1\}
with expectation
\mu
. Then for any
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\geq\left(1+\delta\right)\cdot\mu\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}\mu n}{2+\delta}\right).

Furthermore for any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\leq\left(1-\delta\right)\cdot\mu\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}\mu n}{2}\right).

Lemma A.3

(Azuma-Hoeffding)
.

Let
(Z_{t})_{t\in[T]}
be a sequence of real-valued random variables adapted to filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[T]}
. Suppose that
\lvert Z_{t}\rvert\leq R
almost surely. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}-\En_{t-1}[Z_{t}]\right\rvert\leq R\sqrt{\frac{8\log(2/\delta)}{T}}.

A.2  Information Theory

Our information-theoretic lower bounds are facilitated by recent developments that build a unified framework for
interactive statistical decision making
(ISDM)
[
CFHQRX24
]
. We first state an interactive version of Le Cam’s convex hull method, which can be derived as a consequence of
[
CFHQRX24
]
. For completeness, we include the proof. It closely mirrors the proof of
[
CFHQRX24
]
, which shows how
[
CFHQRX24
]
recovers the noninteractive variant of Le Cam’s convex hull method.

Theorem A.1

(Interactive Le Cam’s Convex Hull Method)
.

For parameter space
\Theta
, let
\mathcal{M}=\{M_{\theta}\mid\theta\in\Theta\}
be a family of models indexed by
\Theta
. Let
\mathcal{Y}
be an observation space. For any fixed
\mathsf{Alg}
and distribution
\nu\in\Delta(\Theta)
, let
\Pr^{\nu,\mathsf{Alg}}\in\Delta(\mathcal{Y})
be defined as the distribution over observations when (1) a parameter is drawn
\theta\sim\nu
, (2) the algorithm interacts with model
M_{\theta}
. Let
L:\Theta\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a loss function. Suppose that
\Theta_{0}\subseteq\Theta
and
\Theta_{1}\subseteq\Theta
are subsets that satisfy the separation condition

\displaystyle L(\theta_{0},y)+L(\theta_{1},y)\geq 2\Delta,\quad\forall\penalty\ y\in\mathcal{Y},\theta_{0}\in\Theta_{0},\theta_{1}\in\Theta_{1}.

for some parameter
\Delta>0
. Then it holds that for any
\mathsf{Alg}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\En_{Y\sim\Pr^{M_{\theta},\mathsf{Alg}}}\left[L(\theta,Y)\right]\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}\max_{\nu_{0}\in\Delta(\Theta_{0}),\nu_{1}\in\Delta(\Theta_{1})}\left(1-D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\Pr^{\nu_{0},\mathsf{Alg}},\Pr^{\nu_{1},\mathsf{Alg}}\right)\right).

(A.1)

Proof.

We will use
[
CFHQRX24
]
with total-variational (TV) distance
D_{f}\coloneqq D_{\mathsf{TV}}
. Define the enlarged model class
\overline{\mathcal{M}}\coloneqq\{M_{\nu}:\nu\in\Delta(\Theta)\}
as well as the loss function extension
\overline{L}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}

\displaystyle\overline{L}(M_{\nu},y)\coloneqq\inf_{\theta\in\mathrm{supp}(\nu)}L(M_{\theta},y).

By the separation condition we have

\displaystyle\overline{L}(M_{\nu_{0}},y)+\overline{L}(M_{\nu_{1}},y)\geq 2\Delta,\quad\forall\penalty\ y\in\mathcal{Y},\nu_{0}\in\Delta(\Theta_{0}),\nu_{1}\in\Delta(\Theta_{1}).

We pick the prior
\mu\coloneqq\mathrm{Unif}(\{M_{\nu_{0}},M_{\nu_{1}}\})
and the reference distribution
\mathbb{Q}\coloneqq\En_{M\sim\mu}[\Pr^{M,\mathsf{Alg}}]
. Observe that

\displaystyle\rho_{\Delta,\mathbb{Q}}\coloneqq\Pr_{M\sim\mu,Y\sim\mathbb{Q}}\left[\overline{L}(M,Y)<\Delta\right]\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Furthermore

\displaystyle\En_{M\sim\mu}\left[D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\Pr^{M,\mathsf{Alg}},\mathbb{Q}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\Pr^{{\nu_{0}},\mathsf{Alg}},\mathbb{Q}\right)+\frac{1}{2}D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\Pr^{{\nu_{1}},\mathsf{Alg}},\mathbb{Q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\Pr^{{\nu_{0}},\mathsf{Alg}},\Pr^{{\nu_{1}},\mathsf{Alg}}\right).

Therefore for any
\delta\in[0,\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}D_{\mathsf{TV}}(\Pr^{{\nu_{0}},\mathsf{Alg}},\Pr^{{\nu_{1}},\mathsf{Alg}}))
we have

\displaystyle\En_{M\sim\mu}\left[D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\Pr^{M,\mathsf{Alg}},\mathbb{Q}\right)\right]\leq\begin{cases}D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\mathrm{Ber}(1-\delta),\mathrm{Ber}(\rho_{\Delta,\mathbb{Q}})\right)&\text{if}\penalty\ \rho_{\Delta,\mathbb{Q}}\leq 1-\delta\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Therefore
[
CFHQRX24
]
gives

\displaystyle\En_{\theta\sim\frac{\nu_{0}+\nu_{1}}{2},Y\sim\Pr^{M_{\theta},\mathsf{Alg}}}\left[L(\theta,Y)\right]\geq\En_{M\sim\mu,Y\sim\Pr^{M,\mathsf{Alg}}}\left[\overline{L}(M,Y)\right]\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}\cdot\left(1-D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\Pr^{{\nu_{0}},\mathsf{Alg}},\Pr^{{\nu_{1}},\mathsf{Alg}}\right)\right).

Taking supremum over
\nu_{0}
and
\nu_{1}
gives the result.
∎

In light of
Theorem
A.1
, we need to analyze the TV distance between an algorithm
\mathsf{Alg}
interactions with two separate environments given by
\nu_{0}
and
\nu_{1}
. The following chain rule lemma will be useful.

Lemma A.4

(Chain Rule for TV Distance, Exercise I.43 of
[
PW25
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}
be any observation space, let
\mathbb{P}^{Z_{n}}
and
\mathbb{Q}^{Z_{n}}
be distributions over
n
-tuples of
\mathcal{Z}
. Then

\displaystyle D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\mathbb{P}^{Z_{n}},\mathbb{Q}^{Z_{n}}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\En_{Z_{1:i-1}\sim\mathbb{P}^{Z_{n}}}\left[D_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\mathbb{P}\left[Z_{i}\mid Z_{1:i-1}\right],\mathbb{Q}\left[Z_{i}\mid Z_{1:i-1}\right]\right)\right].

We also state a form of data-processing inequality for KL divergences.

Lemma A.5

(Lemma 1,
[
GMS19
]
)
.

Consider a measurable space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F})
equipped with two distributions
\mathbb{P}_{1}
and
\mathbb{P}_{2}
. For any
\mathcal{F}
-measurable function
Z:\Omega\mapsto[0,1]
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}{\left(\mathbb{P}_{1}\|\mathbb{P}_{2}\right)}\geq\mathrm{kl}{\left(\En_{1}[Z]\|\En_{2}[Z]\right)}.

A.3  Online Learning

We state a version of the regret guarantee for Online Mirror Descent (OMD). The interested reader is referred to the references
[
Bub11
,
Ora19
]
.

In what follows, denote
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
to be an open convex set, and
\overline{\Omega}
to be its closure.

Definition A.1

(Bregman Divergence)
.

Let
\psi:\overline{\Omega}\to\mathbb{R}
be strictly convex and differentiable on
\Omega
, such that
\lim_{x\to\overline{\Omega}\backslash\Omega}\|\nabla\psi(x)\|=\infty
. Then the
Bregman divergence
with respect to
\psi
is denoted
D_{h}:\overline{\Omega}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
defined as

\displaystyle D_{\psi}(y\|x)\coloneqq\psi(y)-\psi(x)-\langle\nabla\psi(x),y-x\rangle.

The OMD update rule is defined as the following. Fix a non-empty closed convex set
V\subseteq\overline{\Omega}
, initial iterate
x_{1}\in V
, and fixed learning rate
\eta>0
. Suppose at iteration
t\in[T]
we receive loss functions
\ell_{t}:V\to\mathbb{R}
with subdifferentials
g_{t}\in\partial\ell_{t}(x_{t})
. Then the update rule is given by

\displaystyle x_{t+1}\leftarrow\argmin_{x\in V}\langle g_{t},x\rangle+\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\psi}(x\|x_{t}).

OMD enjoys the following regret guarantee.

Theorem A.2
.

Suppose
\psi
is a proper, closed,
\lambda
-strongly convex function with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|
in
V\cap\Omega
, and denote
D_{\psi}
to be its Bregman divergence. Then for any
u\in V
, the following regret bound holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ell_{t}(x_{t})-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ell_{t}(u)\leq\frac{D_{\psi}(u\|x_{1})}{\eta}+\frac{\eta}{2\lambda}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|g_{t}\|_{\star}^{2}.

A.4  Multiclass Learning

Definition A.2

(Natarajan Dimension
[
Nat89
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an instance space and
\mathcal{Y}
be a finite label space. Given a class
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\mathcal{X}}
, we define its
Natarajan dimension
, denoted
\mathrm{Ndim}(\mathcal{H})
, to be the maximum cardinality of a set
C\subseteq\mathcal{X}
that satisfies the following: there exists
h_{0},h_{1}:C\to\mathcal{Y}
such that (1) for all
x\in C
,
h_{0}(x)\neq h_{1}(x)
, and (2) for all
B\subseteq C
, there exists
h\in\mathcal{H}
such that for all
x\in B
,
h(x)=h_{0}(x)
and for all
x\in C\backslash B
,
h(x)=h_{1}(x)
.

A notation we will use throughout is the projection operator. For a hypothesis class
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\mathcal{X}}
and a finite set
X=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathcal{X}^{n}
, we define the projection of
\mathcal{H}
onto
X
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\big|_{X}\coloneqq\left\{\left(h(x_{1}),\cdots,h(x_{n})\right):h\in\mathcal{H}\right\}.

Lemma A.6

(Sauer’s Lemma for Natarajan Classes
[
HL95
]
)
.

Given a hypothesis class
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\mathcal{X}}
with
\lvert\mathcal{Y}\rvert=K
and
\mathrm{Ndim}(\mathcal{H})\leq d
, we have for every
X=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathcal{X}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\mathcal{H}\big|_{X}\Big\rvert\leq\left(\frac{ne(K+1)^{2}}{2d}\right)^{d}.

Theorem A.3

(Multiclass Fundamental Theorem
[
SB14
]
)
.

For any class
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\mathcal{X}}
with
\mathrm{Ndim}(\mathcal{H})=d
and
\lvert\mathcal{Y}\rvert=K
, the minimax sample complexity of
(\varepsilon,\delta)
agnostic PAC learning
\mathcal{H}
can be bounded as

\displaystyle\Omega\left(\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\leq n(\Pi;\varepsilon,\delta)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{d\log K+\log(1/\delta)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right).

Definition A.3

(Pseudodimension)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an instance space. Given a hypothesis class
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{X}}
, its pseudodimension, denoted
\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{H})
, is defined as
\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{H})\coloneqq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H}^{+})
, where
\mathcal{H}^{+}\coloneqq\left\{(x,\theta)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}\left\{h(x)\leq\theta\right\}:h\in\mathcal{H}\right\}
.

Definition A.4

(Covering Numbers)
.

Given a hypothesis class
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{X}}
,
\alpha>0
, and
X=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathcal{X}^{n}
, the covering number
\mathcal{N}_{1}(\mathcal{H},\alpha,X)
is the minimum cardinality of a set
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that for any
h\in\mathcal{H}
there exists a
c\in C
such that
\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lvert h(x_{i})-c_{i}\rvert\leq\alpha
.

Lemma A.7

(
[
JKALS17
]
, see also
[
Pol12
,
LLG96
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}\subset[0,1]^{\mathcal{X}}
be a real-valued hypothesis class, and let
X=\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\right)
be i.i.d. samples drawn from some distribution
\mathcal{D}
on
\mathcal{X}
. Then for any
\alpha>0

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(x_{i})-\En[h(x)]\right\rvert>\alpha\right]\leq 8\En\left[\mathcal{N}_{1}(\mathcal{H},\alpha/8,X)\right]\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{n\alpha^{2}}{128}\right).

Furthermore if
\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{H})\leq d
then we have the bound

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(x_{i})-\En[h(x)]\right\rvert>\alpha\right]\leq 8e(d+1)\left(\frac{16e}{\alpha}\right)^{d}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{n\alpha^{2}}{128}\right),

which is at most
\delta
as long as
n\geq\tfrac{128}{\alpha^{2}}\left(d\log\tfrac{16e}{\alpha}+\log(8e(d+1))+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
.
